Composable with your own conventions. ([jh3ady/claude-plugins](https://github.com/jh3ady/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/legacy-code) · 2 skills)
- **jh3ady-markdown-conventions**: Markdown formatting conventions applied pragmatically, deferring to the project: a composable baseline (CommonMark, the markdownlint rule set, Prettier defaults) for how Claude writes markdown, plus a PostToolUse hook that reformats each edited file with the formatter the project already configures (dprint, Prettier, or markdownlint-cli2) and stays inert when none is set. - **jh3ady-modular-monolith**: The modular monolith applied pragmatically: one deployable unit, strictly bounded modules with public APIs, isolated data, and TypeScript-ecosystem boundary enforcement. - **jh3ady-object-calisthenics**: Jeff Bay's nine rules of object calisthenics applied pragmatically, as a training discipline: one level of indentation, no else, wrap primitives, first-class collections, one dot per line, no abbreviations, small entities, at most two instance variables, and no getters or setters, framed as an exercise to apply strictly and relax with judgement in production, with each rule's underlying principle owned by its sibling plugin (clean-code, solid-principles, refactoring, domain-driven-design). Composable with your own conventions. ([jh3ady/claude-plugins](https://github.com/jh3ady/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/object-calisthenics) · 1 skill)
- **jh3ady-pragmatism**: Pragmatism as the arbiter above every other principle: size the design, architecture, abstraction, process, and technology of a piece of work to its expected lifespan, scale, and reversibility; count the cost of build, carry, delay, and repair; prefer boring, known-failure-mode technology; treat quality as a requirement negotiated with users rather than a developer's private ideal; and never mistake pragmatism for sloppiness (broken windows, tactical tornado). Drawn from The Pragmatic Programmer, A Philosophy of Software Design, Software Engineering at Google, Tidy First?, Choose Boring Techn… ([jh3ady/claude-plugins](https://github.com/jh3ady/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/pragmatism) · 1 skill)
- **jh3ady-refactoring**: Refactoring (Martin Fowler, 2nd edition, with Kent Beck) applied pragmatically, in three skills: the method (the definition, the two hats, why and when to refactor, mechanics in small steps, and self-testing code as a prerequisite), the code smells (the chapter 3 catalogue of bad smells, each pointing to the refactorings that resolve it), and the refactoring catalogue (the full set of moves grouped by category, with condensed mechanics and TypeScript examples). Composable with your own conventions. ([jh3ady/claude-plugins](https://github.com/jh3ady/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/refactoring) · 3 skills)

- **jh3ady-review-conventions**: The Conventional Comments standard for pull and merge request reviews: labelled, decorated, actionable comments. Composable with your own conventions. ([jh3ady/claude-plugins](https://github.com/jh3ady/claude-plugins/tree/HEAD/plugins/review-conventions) · 1 skill)

- **jh3ady-simple-design**: Kent Beck's four rules of simple design applied pragmatically, meta first: the four rules (passes the tests, reveals intention, no duplication, fewest elements) as one priority-ordered decision procedure for the refactor step, how to arbitrate when the rules pull against each other, and simple design as emergent rather than up-front, with each individual rule owned by its sibling plugin (TDD, clean-code, simplicity-principles, refactoring). - **jh3ady-test-driven-development**: Test-driven development applied pragmatically in the classical (Detroit) style: the iron law of a failing test first, red-green-refactor, testing behaviour rather than implementation (the unit is a behaviour or module, not a class), the test-double ladder that prefers real objects and in-memory implementations over stubs and mocks, state verification over interaction verification, keeping in-memory implementations faithful through contract tests, and the inner-loop scope of TDD, with TypeScript specifics.
